\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left(\left\lVert F(\widetilde{t}_{k})-F(s)\right\rVert\big\lVert G(\widetilde{t}_{k})-G(\widetilde{t}_{k-1})\big\rVert\lVert Z(\widetilde{t}_{k})\rVert+\lVert F(s)\rVert\big\lVert G(\widetilde{t}_{k})-G(\widetilde{t}_{k-1})\big\rVert\left\lVert Z(\widetilde{t}_{k})-Z(s)\right\rVert\right)

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon_{1}\sum_{k=1}^{K}(\lVert F(s)\rVert+\lVert Z(\widetilde{t}_{k})\rVert)\big\lVert G(\widetilde{t}_{k})-G(\widetilde{t}_{k-1})\big\rVert\leq\varepsilon_{1}(\lVert F\rVert_{L^{\infty}}+\lVert Z\rVert_{L^{\infty}}){\operatorname{Var}}_{t,t+h}(G),

and therefore

\displaystyle\left\lVert\int_{t}^{t+h}\big(F(\,\mathrm{d}G)Z\big)-F(s)\big(G(t+h)-G(t)\big)Z(s)\right\rVert

\displaystyle\qquad\leq S_{1}+S_{2}\leq\varepsilon_{1}(1+\lVert F\rVert_{L^{\infty}}+\lVert Z\rVert_{L^{\infty}}){\operatorname{Var}}_{t,t+h}(G)\leq\varepsilon{\operatorname{Var}}_{t,t+h}(G),

by choosing
\varepsilon_{1}
appropriately small.
∎

We then have the following result.

Theorem D.13
.

Let
F\in\mathcal{C}([t_{0},t_{1}],\mathbb{C}^{m,n})
,
G\in\BV_{\mathrm{loc}}([t_{0},t_{1}],\mathbb{C}^{n,p})
, and
Z\in\mathcal{C}([t_{0},t_{1}],\mathbb{C}^{p,q})
, and define

H:[t_{0},t_{1}]\to\mathbb{C}^{m,q},\qquad t\mapsto\int_{t_{\textnormal{ref}}}^{t}\big(F(t)\,\mathrm{d}G(t)Z(t)\big),

for a fixed
t_{\textnormal{ref}}\in[t_{0},t_{1}]
.
Then
H\in\BV([t_{0},t_{1}],\mathbb{C}^{m,q})
and
\,\mathrm{d}H=F(\,\mathrm{d}G)Z
, in the sense that

\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\big(L(t)\,\mathrm{d}H(t)M(t)\big)=\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\big(L(t)F(t)\,\mathrm{d}G(t)Z(t)M(t)\big)

(75)

holds for all
L\in\mathcal{C}([t_{0},t_{1}],\mathbb{C}^{l,m})
and
M\in\mathcal{C}([t_{0},t_{1}],\mathbb{C}^{q,r})
.

Proof.

For every partition
\mathfrak{p}=\{\widetilde{t}_{0},\ldots,\widetilde{t}_{K}\}
of
[t_{0},t_{1}]
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\lVert H(\widetilde{t}_{k})-H(\widetilde{t}_{k-1})\rVert=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left\lVert\int_{\widetilde{t}_{k}}^{\widetilde{t}_{k-1}}\big(F(t)\,\mathrm{d}G(t)Z(t)\big)\right\rVert

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\lVert F\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lVert Z\rVert_{L^{\infty}}{\operatorname{Var}}_{\widetilde{t}_{k-1},\widetilde{t}_{k}}(G)\leq\lVert F\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lVert Z\rVert_{L^{\infty}}{\operatorname{Var}}_{t_{0},t_{1}}(G)<\infty,

thus
H\in\BV([t_{0},t_{1}],\mathbb{C}^{m,q})
and all Riemann-Stieltjes integrals are well-defined.

For every
\varepsilon>0
, let
\delta>0
be as in the statement of
Lemma

D.12
.
For every partition
\mathfrak{p}=\{\widetilde{t}_{0},\ldots,\widetilde{t}_{K}\}
of
[t_{0},t_{1}]
with
\lVert\mathfrak{p}\rVert<\delta
we have then

\displaystyle\left\lVert\sum_{k=1}^{K}L(\widetilde{t}_{k})\big(H(\widetilde{t}_{k})-H(\widetilde{t}_{k-1})\big)M(\widetilde{t}_{k})-\sum_{k=1}^{K}L(\widetilde{t}_{k})F(\widetilde{t}_{k})\big(G(\widetilde{t}_{k})-G(\widetilde{t}_{k-1})\big)Z(\widetilde{t}_{k})M(\widetilde{t}_{k})\right\rVert

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\lVert L\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lVert M\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left\lVert\int_{\widetilde{t}_{k-1}}^{\widetilde{t}_{k}}\big(F(t)\,\mathrm{d}G(t)Z(t)\big)-F(\widetilde{t}_{k})\big(G(\widetilde{t}_{k})-G(\widetilde{t}_{k-1})\big)Z(\widetilde{t}_{k})\right\rVert

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\varepsilon\lVert L\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lVert M\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}{\operatorname{Var}}_{\widetilde{t}_{k-1},\widetilde{t}_{k}}(G)\leq\varepsilon\lVert L\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\lVert M\rVert_{L^{\infty}}{\operatorname{Var}}_{t_{0},t_{1}}(G).

Therefore, by taking
\varepsilon
and
\lVert\mathfrak{p}\rVert
arbitrarily small, we conclude that (
75
) holds.
∎

Remark D.14
.

While up to now we have always worked on a compact interval
[t_{0},t_{1}]\subseteq\mathbb{R}
, the definitions and properties of the Riemann-Stieltjes integral extend straightforwardly to open time intervals
\mathbb{T}\subseteq\mathbb{R}
and local function spaces like
\BV_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{m,n})
and
W^{1,1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{m,n})
.
However, the Riemann-Stieltjes integrals are still only taken over compact intervals
[t_{0},t_{1}]\subseteq\mathbb{T}
.

Going back to
Corollary

B.9
, we now show that, when
M\in\BV_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{T},\operatorname{\mathbf{S}}^{n}(\mathbb{C}))
, the solution of (
61
) can be expressed in a more compact way by using the matrix Riemann-Stieltjes integral.

Theorem D.15
.

Let
A\in L^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{n,n})
,
L\in L^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{T},\operatorname{\mathbf{S}}(\mathbb{C}))
,
M\in\BV_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{T},\operatorname{\mathbf{S}}^{n}(\mathbb{C}))
,
t_{0}\in\mathbb{T}
, and
Q_{0}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{S}}^{n}_{+}(\mathbb{C})
. Then the integral equation

Q(t)=Q(t_{0})+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\big({A(s)}^{\mathrm{H}}Q(s)+Q(s)A(s)+L(s)\big)\,\mathrm{d}s+M(t)-M(t_{0}),\qquad Q(t_{0})=Q_{0}

(76)

has a unique global solution
Q\in\BV_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{T},\operatorname{\mathbf{S}}^{n}(\mathbb{C}))
of the form

Q(t)=\Phi_{{A}^{\mathrm{H}}}(t,t_{0})Q_{0}{\Phi_{{A}^{\mathrm{H}}}(t,t_{0})}^{\mathrm{H}}+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\Phi_{{A}^{\mathrm{H}}}(t,s)\big(L(s)\,\mathrm{d}s+\,\mathrm{d}M(s)\big){\Phi_{{A}^{\mathrm{H}}}(t,s)}^{\mathrm{H}}\,\mathrm{d}s,

(77)

where
\Phi_{{A}^{\mathrm{H}}}\in W^{1,1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{T},\operatorname{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{C}))
denotes the state-transition matrix associated to
\dot{x}={A}^{\mathrm{H}}x
.

Proof.

Since
\BV_{\mathrm{loc}}\subseteq L^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}
, because of
Corollary

B.9
we know that (
76
) has a unique solution
Q\in L^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{n,n})
of the form (
62
). Furthermore, since
Q=\widetilde{Q}+M
with
\widetilde{Q}\in W^{1,1}_{\mathrm{loc}}\subseteq\BV_{\mathrm{loc}}
and
M\in\BV_{\mathrm{loc}}
, we deduce that
Q\in\BV_{\mathrm{loc}}
.
By applying the properties of the Riemann-Stieltjes integral, we then obtain that

\displaystyle\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left(\Phi_{{A}^{\mathrm{H}}}(t,s)\,\mathrm{d}M(s){\Phi_{{A}^{\mathrm{H}}}(t,s)}^{\mathrm{H}}\right)=\Phi_{{A}^{\mathrm{H}}}(t,t)M(t){\Phi_{{A}^{\mathrm{H}}}(t,t)}^{\mathrm{H}}-\Phi_{{A}^{\mathrm{H}}}(t,t_{0})M(t_{0}){\Phi_{{A}^{\mathrm{H}}}(t,t_{0})}^{\mathrm{H}}

\displaystyle\qquad-\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left(\frac{\partial\Phi_{{A}^{\mathrm{H}}}}{\partial s}(t,s)M(s)\Phi_{{A}^{\mathrm{H}}}(t,s)+\Phi_{{A}^{\mathrm{H}}}(t,s)M(s)\frac{\partial\Phi_{{A}^{\mathrm{H}}}}{\partial s}(t,s)\right)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\qquad=M(t)-\Phi_{{A}^{\mathrm{H}}}(t,t_{0})M(t_{0}){\Phi_{{A}^{\mathrm{H}}}(t,t_{0})}^{\mathrm{H}}+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\Phi_{{A}^{\mathrm{H}}}(t,s)\big({A(s)}^{\mathrm{H}}M(s)+M(s)A(s)\big){\Phi_{{A}^{\mathrm{H}}}(t,s)}^{\mathrm{H}}\,\mathrm{d}s,

from which it follows that (
62
) and (
77
) are in fact the same expression.
∎

Theorem D.16
.

Let
A_{1}\in\BV_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{p,p})
,
A_{0}\in L^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{n,n})
,
Z\in L^{2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{n,m})
,
M\in L^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{m,m})
, and
V\in\mathcal{C}\cap\BV_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{p,n})
be such that
A_{1}(t)
,
A_{0}(t)
and
M(t)
are self-adjoint for a.e.
t\in\mathbb{T}
.
Then the following statements are equivalent:

(i)

The Riemann-Stieltjes integral matrix inequality

\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\begin{bmatrix}{V(t)}^{\mathrm{H}}\,\mathrm{d}A_{1}(t)V(t)+A_{0}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t&Z(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\\
{Z(t)}^{\mathrm{H}}\,\mathrm{d}t&M(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\end{bmatrix}\geq 0

(78)

holds for all
t_{0},t_{1}\in\mathbb{T},\ t_{0}\leq t_{1}
.

(ii)

The Riemann-Stieltjes integral scalar inequality

\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}{\begin{bmatrix}x(t)\\
u(t)\end{bmatrix}}^{\mathrm{H}}\begin{bmatrix}{V(t)}^{\mathrm{H}}\,\mathrm{d}A_{1}(t)V(t)+A_{0}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t&Z(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\\
{Z(t)}^{\mathrm{H}}\,\mathrm{d}t&M(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}x(t)\\
u(t)\end{bmatrix}\geq 0

(79)

holds for all
x\in\mathcal{C}\cap\BV_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{n})
,
u\in L^{2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{m})
, and
t_{0},t_{1}\in\mathbb{T},\ t_{0}\leq t_{1}
.

(iii)

The Riemann-Stieltjes integral matrix inequality

\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}{\begin{bmatrix}X(t)\\
U(t)\end{bmatrix}}^{\mathrm{H}}\begin{bmatrix}{V(t)}^{\mathrm{H}}\,\mathrm{d}A_{1}(t)V(t)+A_{0}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t&Z(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\\
{Z(t)}^{\mathrm{H}}\,\mathrm{d}t&M(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}X(t)\\
U(t)\end{bmatrix}\geq 0

(80)

holds for all
X\in\mathcal{C}\cap\BV_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{n,q})
,
U\in L^{2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{m,q})
,
t_{0},t_{1}\in\mathbb{T},\ t_{0}\leq t_{1}
, and
q\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof.

(i)
\implies
(ii)

Suppose first that (
78
) holds.
Let
x\in\mathcal{C}\cap\BV_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{n})
,
u\in L^{2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{m})
, and
t_{0},t_{1}\in\mathbb{T},\ t_{0}\leq t_{1}
.
For every
N\in\mathbb{N}
, let us split
[t_{0},t_{1}]
into
N
equal subintervals of length
h_{N}
, i.e.,
\widetilde{t}_{N,k}\coloneqq t_{0}+\frac{k}{N}(t_{1}-t_{0})
for
k=1,\ldots,N
.
We then define
\widetilde{x}_{N,k}\coloneqq x(\widetilde{t}_{N,k-1})
and

\widetilde{u}_{N,k}\coloneqq\frac{1}{h_{N}}\int_{\widetilde{t}_{N,k-1}}^{\widetilde{t}_{N,k}}u(s)\,\mathrm{d}s

for all
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
k=1,\ldots,N
.
For simplicity, let us fix
N\in\mathbb{N}
and remove it from the notation, e.g.
\widetilde{t}_{k}\coloneqq\widetilde{t}_{N,k}
.
Then for
k=1,\ldots,N
it holds that

\int_{\widetilde{t}_{k-1}}^{\widetilde{t}_{k}}\begin{bmatrix}{V}^{\mathrm{H}}\,\mathrm{d}A_{1}V+A_{0}\,\mathrm{d}t&Z\,\mathrm{d}t\\
{Z}^{\mathrm{H}}\,\mathrm{d}t&M\,\mathrm{d}t\end{bmatrix}\geq 0.

Let us now define
\mathbb{T}_{t,N}\coloneqq[\widetilde{t}_{k-1},\widetilde{t}_{k}]
for all
t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
, where
k=k(t)
is the minimum index such that
t\in[\widetilde{t}_{k-1},\widetilde{t}_{k}]
.
We define then

\widetilde{x}_{N}(t)\coloneqq x(\min(\mathbb{T}_{t,N})),\qquad\widetilde{u}_{N}(t)\coloneqq\frac{1}{h_{N}}\int_{\mathbb{T}_{t,N}}u(s)\,\mathrm{d}s,

for all
t\in[t_{0},t_{1}]
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
.
Since
x
is continuous and
[t_{0},t_{1}]
is compact, clearly
\widetilde{x}_{N}\to x|_{[t_{0},t_{1}]}
uniformly.
Furthermore, because of
Theorem

A.5
,
\widetilde{u}_{N}\to u
in
L^{2}([t_{0},t_{1}],\mathbb{C}^{m})
.
Let us then consider the combination

\sum_{k=1}^{N}{\begin{bmatrix}\widetilde{x}_{k}\\
\widetilde{u}_{k}\end{bmatrix}}^{\mathrm{H}}\int_{\widetilde{t}_{k-1}}^{\widetilde{t}_{k}}\begin{bmatrix}{V}^{\mathrm{H}}\,\mathrm{d}A_{1}V+A_{0}\,\mathrm{d}t&Z\,\mathrm{d}t\\
{Z}^{\mathrm{H}}\,\mathrm{d}t&M\,\mathrm{d}t\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\widetilde{x}_{k}\\
\widetilde{u}_{k}\end{bmatrix}\geq 0.

This combination naturally splits into
S_{1}+S_{0}+2\realPart(S_{Z})+S_{M}
, where

\displaystyle S_{1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{k=1}^{N}{\widetilde{x}_{k}}^{\mathrm{H}}\left(\int_{\widetilde{t}_{k-1}}^{\widetilde{t}_{k}}{V(t)}^{\mathrm{H}}\,\mathrm{d}A_{1}(t)V(t)\right)\widetilde{x}_{k},

\displaystyle\qquad S_{0}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{k=1}^{N}\int_{\widetilde{t}_{k-1}}^{\widetilde{t}_{k}}{\widetilde{x}_{k}}^{\mathrm{H}}A_{0}(t)\widetilde{x}_{k}\,\mathrm{d}t,

\displaystyle S_{Z}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{k=1}^{N}\int_{\widetilde{t}_{k-1}}^{\widetilde{t}_{k}}{\widetilde{x}_{k}}^{\mathrm{H}}Z(t)\widetilde{u}_{k}\,\mathrm{d}t,

\displaystyle\qquad S_{M}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{k=1}^{N}\int_{\widetilde{t}_{k-1}}^{\widetilde{t}_{k}}{\widetilde{u}_{k}}^{\mathrm{H}}M(t)\widetilde{u}_{k}\,\mathrm{d}t.

Because of
Theorem

D.13
, it holds that

\lim_{N\to\infty}S_{1}=\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}{x(t)}^{\mathrm{H}}\,\mathrm{d}H(t)x(t)=\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}{x(t)}^{\mathrm{H}}{V(t)}^{\mathrm{H}}\,\mathrm{d}A_{1}(t)V(t)x(t)\,\mathrm{d}t,

with
H:t\mapsto\int_{t_{0}}^{t}{V(s)}^{\mathrm{H}}\,\mathrm{d}A_{1}(s)V(s)\,\mathrm{d}s
.
Furthermore, since we can write equivalently

\displaystyle S_{0}

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}{\widetilde{x}_{N}(t)}^{\mathrm{H}}A_{0}(t)\widetilde{x}_{N}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t,

\displaystyle S_{Z}

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}{\widetilde{x}_{N}(t)}^{\mathrm{H}}Z(t)\widetilde{u}_{N}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t,

\displaystyle S_{M}

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}{\widetilde{u}_{N}(t)}^{\mathrm{H}}M(t)\widetilde{u}_{N}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t,

with the generalized Hölder inequality (
Theorem

A.1
) we deduce that

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}S_{0}

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}{x}^{\mathrm{H}}(t)A_{0}(t)x(t)\,\mathrm{d}t,

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}S_{Z}

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}{x}^{\mathrm{H}}(t)Z(t)u(t)\,\mathrm{d}t,

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}S_{M}

\displaystyle=\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}{u}^{\mathrm{H}}(t)M(t)u(t)\,\mathrm{d}t.

Thus, in the limit, we obtain exactly (
79
).

(ii)
\implies
(iii)

Suppose now that (
79
) holds.
Let
X\in\mathcal{C}\cap BV_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{n,q})
,
U\in L^{2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{m,q})
,
t_{0},t_{1}\in\mathbb{T},\ t_{0}\leq t_{1}
, and
q\in\mathbb{N}
.
For every
v\in\mathbb{C}^{n+m}
it holds that

\displaystyle{v}^{\mathrm{H}}\left(\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}{\begin{bmatrix}X(t)\\
U(t)\end{bmatrix}}^{\mathrm{H}}\begin{bmatrix}{V(t)}^{\mathrm{H}}\,\mathrm{d}A_{1}(t)V(t)+A_{0}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t&Z(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\\
{Z(t)}^{\mathrm{H}}\,\mathrm{d}t&M(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}X(t)\\
U(t)\end{bmatrix}\right)v=

\displaystyle\qquad=\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}{\begin{bmatrix}X(t)v\\
U(t)v\end{bmatrix}}^{\mathrm{H}}\begin{bmatrix}{V(t)}^{\mathrm{H}}\,\mathrm{d}A_{1}(t)V(t)+A_{0}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t&Z(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\\
{Z(t)}^{\mathrm{H}}\,\mathrm{d}t&M(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}X(t)v\\
U(t)v\end{bmatrix}\geq 0,

since
x\coloneqq Xv\in\mathcal{C}\cap BV_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{n})
and
u\coloneqq Uv\in L^{2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{m})
. We deduce that (
80
) is satisfied.

(iii)
\implies
(i)

This follows immediately by choosing for
X
and
U
constant matrices such that

\begin{bmatrix}X(t)\\
U(t)\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}I_{n}&0\\
0&I_{m}\end{bmatrix},

for all
t\in\mathbb{T}
. ∎

Corollary D.17
.

Let
G\in\BV_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{T},\operatorname{\mathbf{S}}^{n}(\mathbb{C}))
.
Then
G
is weakly increasing if and only if
\,\mathrm{d}G\geq 0
, in the sense that

\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}{X(t)}^{\mathrm{H}}\,\mathrm{d}G(t)X(t)\geq 0

holds for all
X\in\mathcal{C}\cap\BV_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{n,q})
. Similarly,
G
weakly decreases if and only if
\,\mathrm{d}G\leq 0
.

Proof.

By applying
Theorem

D.16
with
m=0
and
V=I_{n}
, we obtain that the inequalities

G(t_{1})-G(t_{0})=\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}\,\mathrm{d}G(t)\geq 0,\qquad\text{for all }t_{0},t_{1}\in\mathbb{T},\ t_{0}\leq t_{1}

and

\int_{t_{0}}^{t_{1}}{X(t)}^{\mathrm{H}}\,\mathrm{d}G(t)X(t)\geq 0,\qquad\text{for all $X\in\mathcal{C}\cap\BV_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{C}^{n,q})$ and }t_{0},t_{1}\in\mathbb{T},\ t_{0}\leq t_{1}

are equivalent. In particular,
G
increases monotonically if and only if
\,\mathrm{d}G\geq 0
.
To show the second part of the statement, it suffices to apply the first part to
-G
and
\,\mathrm{d}(-G)=-\,\mathrm{d}G
.
∎
